# What kppc compiles: the specification suite

Generated by `compiler\tests\spec.cmd` from `compiler/tests/spec/<area>/*.kpp`: one small program per
construct, compiled, run and compared with its `.expected` output. The Factorio column is the
construct's count in the game's sources (`src`, `CommonUtil`, `Agui`; 7,371 files), so the gaps are
ordered by what the conversion needs. A pending row is a known gap and shows the compiler's first
complaint; it turns into a failure of the suite the day it starts passing, so the row gets removed.

**1061 pass, 10 pending, 0 failing, 0 fixed** (1071 snippets, 79.5 s).

## Language: statements, expressions, declarations

| Snippet | Construct | Status | Factorio | Note |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `language/array_parameter_is_pointer.kpp` | a parameter declared as an array is a pointer to its element (C's and C++'s adjustment): `void f(unsigned rep[3])` is `void f(unsigned* rep)`, so a pointer is an argument for it; and in a function pointer type too: `unsigned (*)(unsigned rep[3])` is `unsigned (*)(unsigned*)` | pending | zstd (converted as C): ZSTD_blockCompressor's `U32 rep[ZSTD_REP_NUM]` parameter, with the functions of that pointer type taking `U32 rep[ZSTD_REP_NUM]` and called with `zc->blockState.nextCBlock->rep` decayed; the converter writes `U32* rep` until this passes | the parameter stays an array: "a pointer (...(uint32*)...) does not convert to ...(uint32[3])...", "codegen does not support a conversion from uint32* to uint32[3]" (D); C:\kpp-sandbox\worktrees\codegen\compiler\tests\spec\language\array_parameter_is_pointer.kpp:16: error: a pointer (uint32(uint32*)*) does not convert to uint32(uint32[3])* without a cast (in @kpp.main) |

| `language/const_value_assigned.kpp` | a const value is never assigned: `r = 5` through a `const int&`, a const local's `c += 1`, a `const volatile` local's `cv = 4` and `cb.get() = 7` through a const getter are errors, as in C++ | pass | found by the templates thread with language/non_const_member_overload_for_non_const_object: `history[key] = 1` through the const operator[]'s `const int&` was accepted and stored (silent); the check flagged 101 valid game lines until `volatile T` stopped being typed `const volatile T` (language/volatile_is_not_const) |  |
| `language/const_value_incremented.kpp` | a const value is never incremented, decremented or assigned as a whole: `++c`, `c--` on a const int, `++*p` through a `const int*` and the implicit copy assignment of a const object are errors, as in C++ ([expr.pre.incr], [class.copy.assign]) | pass | found by the templates thread with language/const_value_assigned: each was accepted and stored (silent) |  |
| `language/constant_calls_plain_function_defined_later.kpp` | a constant may call a plain function defined after it (kpp's symbols are order-independent): the evaluator runs the function as a walk of it types it, made for the evaluation when its file's walk has not reached it yet (or is in it on another thread), so `pick(v)` is pick(int), never the first pick of the name | pass | found by the templates thread answering thread A (are plain functions safe to evaluate while their file is walked?): before, the evaluator read the function's own nodes, unresolved above their walk, and ran the overload set's first pick, so `int sized[twice(3)]` had 200 elements while twice(3) is 6 |  |
| `language/constant_data_globals.kpp` | a global whose initializer the compiler evaluates is constant data, built by no startup code: a class with a constexpr constructor, bit fields, an aggregate holding an array of classes, a string and a double, a pointer to another global and to a function, an array, a pointer into it; each reads back its value | pass |  |  |
| `language/constant_reads_initializer_defined_later.kpp` | a constant read before its definition, or from another file (kpp's symbols are order-independent), is the value of its initializer as a walk of it types it: `pick(3)` in k's initializer is pick(int), in a lambda called there, in a data member's default and in an enumerator's value too, whatever order the files are walked in | pass | found by the templates thread checking compile-time lambdas for thread A: with k = pick(3) in another file, `int sized[k]` had 100 or 4 elements by the order the files were walked in and the thread count; the evaluator read the initializer's own nodes, unresolved above their file's walk |  |
| `language/constant_template_calls.kpp` | a constant expression calling a function template without written template arguments (`max(a, b)`: deduced from the arguments' types), and a type's name called with one value (`size_t(5)` through an alias, `uint8_t(300)`), evaluate as template arguments and in static_asserts | pass | PropertyTree's `using KeyType = ImmutableStringTemplate<::max(sizeof(SharedPtr<const String>) + 1u, 2 * sizeof(const char *) - 1)>` |  |
| `language/consteval_addresses.kpp` | addresses at compile time: of a global, of an element of one, of a function (written with & or decaying), and a pointer to a data member; read through, compared, indexed, called through | pass | function pointer tables (zlib_filefunc64_def_s's fopen64_file_func), RailPrototype's constant accessor arrays of member pointers |  |
| `language/consteval_arrays_and_strings.kpp` | constant arrays at compile time: of numbers (a C array and the library's Array by brace elision, elements by index and through a pointer), of objects, string literals and the library's StringView over them (its length counted with strlen) | pass | static_assert(!variantNames[size_t(TileTransitionVariant::Count) - 1].empty()) with `constexpr static inline Array<StringView, 6> variantNames = {"inner_corner", ...}` |  |
| `language/consteval_assignment_operator_runs_on_both_objects.kpp` | a class's assignment operator at compile time runs on the assigned object where it is, with the right operand where it is (an lvalue's object, a prvalue as a temporary): a move assignment that changes its source changes it, and `this == &other` compares the two objects; the constant equals what the program computes (`1 1 2`) | pass | found by the templates thread measuring build.kpp (stage 7): String's `operator=(String&&)` compares `this == &other`, and its move must empty the source (else two Strings own one block) |  |

| `language/variadic_calls_are_unchecked.kpp` | a C-variadic call is never format-checked (design.md 14, kovarex 2026-11-01: kpp prints through print/toString and the converter rewrites printf-family calls; what stays variadic is the C boundary): more arguments than the format's conversions compile, the extra ones unread as in C (`1 2|7`) | pass | replaces language/printf_format_must_match_arguments and scanf_format_must_match_arguments, the retired check's specs |  |
| `language/variadic_function_pointer_type.kpp` | a function pointer type written with `...` is the type of a pointer to a variadic function, distinct from the same type without it, as in C++ | pass | the library's printf, fprintf, snprintf, sprintf and sscanf against their pointer types (library/c_declarations_as_in_c_headers) |  |
| `language/variadic_with_own_format.kpp` | a C-variadic function whose last named parameter is a `const char*` but which is not printf-like reads its arguments by its own format; no C-variadic call is format-checked (design.md 14), and every argument is passed | pass | stb_image (converted as C): stb_image_write's `stbiw__writef(s, "111 221 2222 11", 0, 0, format, 0, 0, 0, 0, 0, x, y, bits, alpha)` and stbiw__outfile, which write TGA's and BMP's headers by the digits of their format (the converter had dropped the arguments to satisfy the printf check: the headers came out as garbage) |  |
| `language/void_returned_as_reference.kpp` | a function that returns a reference cannot return an expression without a value (a call of a function returning void): an error, as in C++ | pass |  |  |
| `language/volatile_is_not_const.kpp` | `volatile` alone qualifies a type as volatile, not const: a volatile object, a member written through a `volatile T*` and a `T* volatile` pointer are assigned as in C++; `const volatile` is both | pass | the game writes vertices through `volatile VertexLayout::Sprite*` buffers (DrawCommandBatch, SpriteDrawOrder) and Lua's `L->errorJmp->status` is a `volatile int`: kppc typed every `volatile T` as `const volatile T`, which an assignment check flagged at 101 valid lines |  |
| `language/wide_strings.kpp` | wide string literals and WString | pass | 44 uses in 10 files (literals), 58 (std::wstring) |  |

| `library/align_negative_width.kpp` | alignRight and alignLeft with a negative width align left in that many characters, as printf's `*` width taken from an argument does (`printf("%*d", -4, 7)` is "7   "); a positive width is as before | pass | LuaHelper's `printf("%*c{\n", depth, ' ')`, written alignRight(char(' '), depth) by the printf rewrite (design.md 14) |  |

| `library/unsigned_size_traps.kpp` | the library's sizes are unsigned (ContainerSize), and its loops and subtractions are right at the edges where a signed size went below 0: a backward search that reaches index 0 (and stops there, or on an empty text), a needle longer than the text (no length - count that wraps), npos as "to the end" in substr and erase, the iterator difference of a Deque that is negative, Deque's erase of a range at the front, a heap of 0, 1 and 2 elements, a comparison result that is negative, an extension at a name's first character | pass | every container and string the game uses; the traps of docs/stdlib-conformance.md, "Sizes are unsigned" |  |
| `library/utf8.kpp` | UTF-8 (docs/stdlib/utf8.md): decoding gives U+FFFD for each maximal subpart of bytes that are not UTF-8, as the Unicode Standard's table 3-8 shows (61 F1 80 80 E1 80 C2 62 80 63 80 BF 64 is a, three U+FFFD, b, U+FFFD, c, two U+FFFD, d); overlong forms, surrogates, code points past U+10FFFF and a sequence cut short are not UTF-8; decoding backwards meets the code points decoding forwards does; encoding writes the shortest form and throws InvalidArgument for a code point that is no scalar value; isUtf8, utf8InvalidAt and utf8Length agree with the decoding | pass | none; json, html, markdown and url (libraries/), which each had a copy |  |
| `library/variant_free_functions_by_type.kpp` | holdsAlternative<T>(u), get<T>(u) and getIf<T>(&u) by type on a converted std::variant (a typed union with the converter's holds<T>, get<T> and getIf<T>), beside get<I> of a Tuple and a Pair by index | pass | the tests' TestSegmentedUnit, `CHECK_EQUAL(std::get<SegmentedUnitAI::Attacking>(ai.getState()).target.get(), belt)` and std::holds_alternative on the AI's state, in test bodies the converter does not parse (UnitTest++'s macros make them) |  |
| `library/vector.kpp` | Vector (std::vector): push/emplace, index, erase, insert, iteration, nesting, moves, sort | pass | 5225 uses in 1662 files |  |
| `library/vector_brace_init.kpp` | Vector from a brace list | pass | common for constant tables (std::vector<T> v = {...}) |  |
| `library/vector_equality.kpp` | two Vectors are equal when they have the same length and equal elements (`==` and `!=`) | pass |  |  |
| `library/vector_growth_move_if_noexcept.kpp` | a Vector's reallocation (growth, reserve) moves an element only when its move constructor cannot throw or it cannot be copied, and copies it otherwise (std::vector's move_if_noexcept, for the strong exception guarantee): a copyable type with a noexcept(false) move is copied, one with a noexcept move is moved, a move-only one is moved; an insert's shift still moves | pass | Sprite's `Sprite(Sprite&&) noexcept(false)`: a std::vector<Sprite> copies its sprites when it grows in the C++ build |  |
| `library/vector_insert_range.kpp` | Vector::insert(position, first, last) from input iterators of any kind (pointers, a List's, a single-pass one, MoveIterator moving move-only elements), insert of a brace list and of count copies, at const positions (std::vector::insert taking const_iterator) | pass | SmallVector::switchToBigData (`insert(end(), MoveIterator(begin()), MoveIterator(end()))`), range inserts across Util and the GUI |  |
| `library/views.kpp` | the views: iotaView piped into transformView, transformView of a container held by reference, reverseView of a view, Subrange of two pointers; and parseTime reading an ISO date-time | pass | ChunkLineByLineScan (iota | transform), TerritoryManager (transform of the territories, reversed), ComplexNoiseExpressions (ranges::subrange), TimeUtil's fromISODate (get_time) |  |
| `library/wstring_insert.kpp` | WString's insert before an index of a WString, a zero-terminated text, count copies of a unit and part of itself, as std::wstring inserts them; an index past the end throws OutOfRange | pass | the tests' ForkTask.cpp, `params.insert(0, executablePath + L" ")` on the command line it hands CreateProcessW |  |

## kpp's own features (design.md sections 7 and 8)

| Snippet | Construct | Status | Factorio | Note |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `kpp/when_member_of_class_template_per_instance.kpp` | a `when` member of a class template whose condition depends on T (design.md 19) is in the layout of the instances where it holds and absent from the others: Slot<int> keeps a backup copy, Slot<Handle> (a Handle has a destructor of its own, so it is not trivially copyable) has none and its member's type is never checked | pending | the game's class templates under #ifdef DEBUG members, and type-dependent members such as the trivially copyable cases of its containers | `when` is not parsed yet (A: the parser, the scope and the layout; D: the uses and the per-instance condition); C:\kpp-sandbox\worktrees\codegen\compiler\tests\spec\kpp\when_member_of_class_template_per_instance.kpp:14: error: a when at namespace or class scope needs a condition made of config constants and literals (design.md 19): a member per class template instance is not supported yet (at 'isTriviallyCopyable') |
| `kpp/asserts_and_config.kpp` | assert and release_assert statements, config constants with if constexpr, conditional functions, CallerLocation | pass |  |  |
| `kpp/attributes_inline.kpp` | [[noinline]] and [[forceinline]] attributes | pass |  |  |
| `kpp/auto_return_through_for_constexpr.kpp` | an auto return type deduced from returns inside a `for constexpr` body, as if the body were written out once per element: `visitUnion(f, u)` over alternatives(U) returns f's result; a list of types (`sizeof(E)` of its type variable included) and a list of constants alike | pass |  |  |
| `kpp/config_constant_per_configuration.kpp` | a constant whose value differs per configuration is one declaration whose initializer chooses by the configuration (design.md 19: `constexpr i32 ROW_STEP = config::debug ? 4 : 2;`): a constant expression in each, usable in static_assert, and the statements after it see the configuration's value, as C++'s `#ifdef DEBUG ... #else ... #endif` declared it | pass | FogShaderRenderLogic's `constexpr int32_t ROW_STEP` (4 under DEBUG, 2 otherwise) and TestNoisePerformance's `static constexpr uint32_t ITERATIONS` (16, 128), each used after its #endif (design.md 12, "The game's DEBUG as config::debug") |  |
| `kpp/config_debug_branch_names_debug_only_members.kpp` | a discarded `if constexpr (config::debug)` branch in a function body is not name-checked: it may name members, functions and types that only the debug configuration declares (at class and namespace scope, inside their own `if constexpr (config::debug)`), as the C++ preprocessor's `#ifdef DEBUG` lets it; the kept branch is checked as always | pass | kovarex 2026-11-02, "we need to have debug and release configuration working properly": the game's 392 DEBUG conditionals (273 in function bodies, 61 in class bodies, 57 at namespace scope) as `if constexpr (config::debug)`, TransportLine's debug-only referenceSet among them (design.md 12) |  |
| `kpp/config_library_scope.kpp` | a config constant in a library's scope (build.kpp's library options: `--config zlib::debug=true`) is config::zlib::debug, read in an expression, in a declaration-scope if constexpr and in conditional(), apart from the program's own config::debug (`true 7 checked on fast`) | pass |  |  |
| `kpp/config_values.kpp` | config constants from the command line (`--config name=value`, `--release`) as compile-time values | pass |  |  |
| `kpp/enum_count_as_text_piece.kpp` | a real enum's generated `count` is a value as a piece of a Text, as anywhere: `println(Direction::count)` prints the number of values | pass |  |  |
| `kpp/enum_from_str.kpp` | Direction::fromStr returning an Optional | pass |  |  |
| `kpp/extern_c_variable_in_library.kpp` | an extern "C" variable a library declares is the C variable of that name, not one in the library's scope (design.md 16) | pass |  |  |
| `kpp/for_constexpr_constant.kpp` | the for constexpr value is a compile-time constant: a template argument, an array bound | pass |  |  |
| `kpp/for_constexpr_switch.kpp` | for constexpr inside a switch, one case per type (design.md 8.2) | pass |  |  |
| `kpp/for_constexpr_types.kpp` | for constexpr over a list of types | pass |  |  |
| `kpp/for_constexpr_values.kpp` | for constexpr over a list of constants | pass |  |  |
| `kpp/implicit_constructors.kpp` | `implicit` constructors convert, others do not (kpp's default is explicit) | pass |  |  |
| `kpp/library_constants_and_functions_of_c_macros.kpp` | a scoped library's interface converted from C macros (design.md 16, "As converted"): its number macros are constants of its scope with the macro's value and type (zstd::ZSTD_CONTENTSIZE_UNKNOWN is an unsigned long long, ZSTD_MAGICNUMBER an unsigned int, ZSTD_STRATEGY_MAX the enumeration's), and a function-like macro of values a function whose arithmetic is the macro's on the argument's type (an int argument gives an int, a size_t a size_t, a negative one the macro's value) | pass | zstd's interface (zstd.h), which the game writes zstd::ZSTD_COMPRESSBOUND, zstd::ZSTD_CONTENTSIZE_ERROR; programs/factorio/harness/zstd.c checks every one against the C build |  |
| `kpp/library_declares_a_name_twice_is_error.kpp` | inside one scoped library two declarations of one name in two of its files are the global-clash error (design.md 16), as two of the program's are | pass | none yet |  |
| `kpp/library_name_taken_by_the_program_is_error.kpp` | a scoped library's name is the program's qualifier for it (design.md 16): a namespace of the program or a global of that name is an error, never a silent merge with the library's names | pass | none yet |  |
| `kpp/library_name_used_bare_is_error.kpp` | a scoped library's name written bare outside the library is not declared, and the error names the qualified one (design.md 16: "did you mean zlib::compress?"), for a function, a type and a constant | pass | none yet |  |
| `kpp/library_names_are_qualified.kpp` | a scoped library's top-level names are qualified by the library's name (design.md 16): the program writes zlib::compress, zlib::Stream, zlib::Mode::best, zlib::twice<int>, zlib::detail::depth; inside the library they are bare, its private names are its files', and argument-dependent lookup finds a library class's functions | pass | none yet; the converted C libraries (zlib, ogg, ...) are to be scoped by their names, the converter qualifying the game's calls into them |  |
| `kpp/nameof.kpp` | nameof(expr) as a string literal | pass |  |  |
| `kpp/no_includes_order_independence.kpp` | no includes, declaration order does not matter: uses before definitions, mutually recursive types | pass |  |  |
| `kpp/private_instances_and_vtables_of_two_files.kpp` | two files' private class templates and function templates of one name instantiated with the same arguments, and private classes of one name with virtual functions and a static data member, are each file's own (their instances, vtables and statics apart); a global template calling its file's private helper does so wherever it is instantiated, beside another file's private helper of that name | pass | the converted C libraries' file-local helpers (design.md 15), called from macros-turned-templates and from functions the game's files instantiate |  |
| `kpp/private_name_declared_twice_in_its_file_is_error.kpp` | two declarations of one name private to one file are a clash, as two globals are ("top-level names are global"): a file's private names are one scope of their own; and `private` is for a file's top-level names: inside a namespace it is an error, not a global name | pass | none; the rule beside kpp/private_names_of_two_files_do_not_clash |  |
| `kpp/private_name_from_another_file_is_error.kpp` | naming another file's `private` name is an error that says whose it is (design.md 15: "helper is private to zlib/inflate.kpp"), for a function, a type and a constant | pass | none yet; the converted C libraries' `static` names, once private, are named only in their own files |  |
| `kpp/private_name_hides_a_global.kpp` | a top-level declaration marked `private` is the file's own (design.md 15): in its file it hides a global name of the same spelling, from the file's functions, its global classes' members, its namespaces' functions and `::name`, while every other file sees the global | pass | C's file-level `static` (16,900 file-local declarations in the 15 C libraries, design.md 15) and the game's 7 anonymous namespaces, which the converter maps to `private` |  |
| `kpp/private_names_of_two_files_do_not_clash.kpp` | two files' `private` names of one spelling never clash (design.md 15): each file has its own function, constant, variable and type of the name, with its own layout, and the program has both | pass | SDL3 reuses 316 file-local names across its files, curl 82, jpeg 28, zstd 26, Lua 24 (design.md 15), which the converter renamed one by one before |  |
| `kpp/private_types_and_templates.kpp` | `private` on every kind of top-level declaration: a class with members defined out of line, a class template, a function template, an enum and an enum class, an alias, a constexpr constant and a variable; each the file's own, beside another file's global of the same name and another shape | pass | the game's anonymous namespaces hold classes and templates (Atlas.cpp, AtlasBuilder.cpp, PipelineStateObjectOpenGL.cpp), which the converter maps to `private` |  |
| `kpp/private_variable_in_extern_c_is_error.kpp` | a `private` variable cannot have C linkage (design.md 15): its name is its file's, and C's names are the program's, so `private` inside `extern "C" { }` is an error | pass |  |  |
| `kpp/real_enums.kpp` | real enums: value block, methods with this as the value, count, values, str, exhaustive switch, nameof | pass |  |  |
| `kpp/reflection_bases_offsets.kpp` | members(T) lists the bases' members first; m.offset; object.[m] is assignable | pass |  |  |
| `kpp/reflection_members.kpp` | members(T) with m.name, m.type and object.[m] | pass |  |  |
| `kpp/requires_file.kpp` | `requires <condition>;` at the top of a file (compiled only when the config says so) | pass |  |  |
| `kpp/run_command.kpp` | `kppc --run file.kpp args...` builds the program, runs it with the arguments and returns its exit code | pass |  |  |
| `kpp/test_build_error.kpp` | kppc --test runs nothing when the build printed an error, whatever its exit code (a test the parser could not read would silently not run) | pass |  |  |
| `kpp/test_check_values.kpp` | a failing check prints its location, its text and a comparison's operand values, and the test goes on | pass |  |  |
| `kpp/test_exception.kpp` | an exception that leaves a test fails that test; one derived from Exception shows its message | pass |  |  |
| `kpp/test_filter.kpp` | the runner's --filter runs the tests whose full name ("file > name") contains the text, `*` matching anything | pass |  |  |
| `kpp/test_in_class.kpp` | a test inside a class sees the class's private members; it is the class's static function | pass |  |  |
| `kpp/test_list.kpp` | the runner's --list prints every test's full name and location without running any | pass |  |  |
| `kpp/test_report.kpp` | the runner's --report FILE (- for stdout) writes one JSON object per test and a summary, for editors and test explorers | pass |  |  |
| `kpp/test_require.kpp` | require ends the test when it fails, a failing release_assert (an assert in a debug build) inside a test fails that test, and the run goes on; check and require are never compiled out | pass |  |  |
| `kpp/test_runner.kpp` | `kppc --test file.kpp` builds the file's `test "name" { }` declarations with a runner in place of main and runs them all | pass |  |  |
| `kpp/tests_in_compiler.kpp` | `test "name" { check(...); }` declarations are accepted and cost nothing without --tests | pass |  |  |
| `kpp/two_libraries_declare_one_name.kpp` | two scoped libraries and the program declare one name each (design.md 16: adding a scoped library never changes or breaks existing code): no clash, and each library's functions, globals, classes with their vtables and out-of-line members, and template instances are its own; a library names another's qualified | pass | none yet; zlib and minizip, ogg and vorbis, once each is a scoped library |  |
| `kpp/typed_union_alternative_built_in_place.kpp` | assigning a temporary to a typed union's alternative of a fixed place (`target[a] = a.type(input)` with target a reference, a an alternative) builds the alternative in place from the constructor's arguments: no move, whatever C++17's order of the assignment's operands (the place does not depend on the right operand) | pass | DeserialiserHelper::loadSTDVariant (overrides) loading SegmentedUnitAI::AIState: Targeter's move constructor asserts the targeter is not being loaded, and the menu simulation vulcanus-punishment and the Simulations unit tests stopped there when the assignment's right operand was first made a temporary and moved from |  |
| `kpp/typed_union_alternatives.kpp` | `alternatives(U)` reflection over a typed union: `a.name`, `a.type`, `a.tag`, `u[a]`, `u == a.tag`, and `u.tag()`, enough to save the alternative held and load one by its index | pass |  |  |
| `kpp/typed_union_comparisons.kpp` | typed unions compare as std::variant does: == when both hold the same alternative and its values are equal; < (and the other orderings) by the alternative held, in declaration order, then by the values; a defaulted <=> of a class with a typed union member orders it so | pass | UndoActionDescription's defaulted <=> over its ValueVariant (std::variant converted to a typed union) |  |
| `kpp/typed_union_direct_init.kpp` | a typed union direct-initialized from a value of exactly one alternative's type, as copy-initialization is: `U u(x)`, `U(x)`, a member initializer `state(x)`, and placement new `new (slot) U(x)` (what Vector::emplace_back(x) does) | pass | DrawCommandBatch's `batches.emplace_back(desc)` and `emplace_back(std::in_place_type_t<SpriteBatchDescription>(), ...)` (std::variant's converting constructor), ZoomSpecification's constructors initializing its variant member from a ZoomVariant or a ViewDistanceVariant |  |
| `kpp/typed_union_from_descriptor_tag.kpp` | in a `for constexpr` body over `alternatives(U)`, a descriptor's `a.tag` is a constant tag of U, so `U(a.tag, value)` constructs that alternative (design.md 8.5): loading a union by its saved index picks the alternative whichever others share its type | pass | the converter's DeserialiserHelper override for std::variant (loadSTDVariantIndirect): `return T(a.tag, input.template load<a.type>());` |  |
| `kpp/typed_union_from_tag_and_value.kpp` | a typed union constructed from a tag and the alternative's value or constructor arguments (design.md 8.5, decided by kovarex 2026-10-31): `U(U::Text, x)`, `U u(U::Int2, 7);`, `return U(U::Comment, s);` and `U u(U::Where, 1, 2)` build that alternative directly from them, whichever other alternatives share its type, and with no default construction of the first alternative first (here one with no default constructor) | pass | InputAction's many actions with the same data told apart by the tag, which a value alone cannot choose (the last left-out unit test); the converter maps such a variant construction to this form |  |
| `kpp/typed_union_from_tag_and_value_errors.kpp` | constructing a typed union from a tag needs a constant tag, one of its alternatives (a tag read at run time cannot choose the alternative to build); a value alone whose type two alternatives share stays an ambiguity error, as before (design.md 8.5, decided by kovarex 2026-10-31) | pass |  |  |
| `kpp/typed_union_from_value.kpp` | a typed union constructed and assigned from a value of exactly one alternative's type: `AIState s = Patrolling{1}`, `return Attacking{n}`, `s = Attacking{7}` | pass |  |  |
| `kpp/typed_union_trait.kpp` | isTypedUnion<T>, whether T is a typed union (`union enum`), a constant like the other type traits (std has none; a std::variant check is the nearest) | pass | the game's is_std_variant<T>, which picks the variant save and load in SerialiserHelper and DeserialiserHelper for the 9 std::variants that become typed unions |  |
| `kpp/typed_union_triviality_follows_alternatives.kpp` | a typed union is trivially copyable or trivially destructible only when every alternative is: with an alternative holding a member whose copy constructor and destructor are user-provided, isTriviallyCopyable<State> and isTriviallyDestructible<State> are false, so Optional<State> keeps its value as an object, copied through the alternative's constructors and destroyed by its destructor, as std::optional<std::variant<...>> does | pass | SegmentedUnitAI's `if (const Optional<AIState> newState = this->maybeEnrage())`, AIState a std::variant whose Attacking holds a Targeter<Entity>: the Optional took the union as trivially copyable and never destroyed its value, so the Targeter stayed in its target's list after the storage was gone, and the list broke later: faults in Targeters::clear, unlinkTargeter and notifyUndoActionsOfEntityDestroyed stopped Achievements, SegmentedUnit, LuaSegmentedUnit, LuaEventFilters and Simulations, all of which pass with the alternatives counted as the union's fields (fieldTypes) |  |

| `kpp/underscore_locals.kpp` | `_` as a repeatable unnamed local living to the end of its scope | pass |  |  |

| `kpp/when_false_name_hides_outer_names.kpp` | where a `when` condition is false (design.md 19), its declaration's name still hides the names of the same spelling around it: a use outside the condition is an error, never a use of the global `tally`, the base's `size`, the outer `Info` or the outer `note` | pass | none: the converter writes each use of a debug-only name inside `if constexpr (config::debug)` (the game's #ifdef DEBUG members and globals beside release ones of the same spelling) |  |
| `kpp/when_in_template_decided_per_instance.kpp` | a `when` condition on a template's types (design.md 19) is decided per instance: the declaration exists in the instances where it holds, and in the others there is none and its initializer is not checked (a Handle, whose destructor is its own, is no T to copy here) | pass | the game's templates over trivially copyable types (`std::is_trivially_copyable_v<T>`), and #ifdef DEBUG inside templates converted to `when (config::debug)` |  |
| `kpp/when_local_used_by_later_if_constexpr.kpp` | a local declared with `when (config::debug)` (design.md 19) is the enclosing block's, so a later `if constexpr (config::debug)` in the block uses it, as C++'s two `#ifdef DEBUG` sections share a local; one declared `when (!config::debug)` alike in the `else` of that `if constexpr`, where its condition is known to hold | pass | LogisticManager's `std::vector<LogisticCell*> neighbors` and DeliverJob's `LogisticPoint* point`, LatencyOneTimeEffect's `itTick`, TextureCopyHelperDX11's `sourceWidth` and FogShaderRenderLogic's `cachedTileID`, each declared under one #ifdef DEBUG and used under a later one (design.md 12) |  |
| `kpp/when_member_exists_per_configuration.kpp` | a `when` class member (design.md 19) is part of the class's layout only where its condition holds: a debug-only statistics member makes the class larger in debug and is absent in release, and a constructor's member initializer for it exists exactly when the member does (in release `lookups(5)` names no member and is not checked) | pass | the game's 61 class members under #ifdef DEBUG (statistics and checks), converted to `when (config::debug)` (design.md 12) |  |
| `kpp/when_namespace_variable.kpp` | a `when` declaration at namespace scope (design.md 19) exists only where its condition holds: a debug-only global counter in a namespace and at file scope, used inside `if constexpr (config::debug)`; in release there is no variable and its initializer is not checked | pass | the game's 57 namespace-scope declarations under #ifdef DEBUG (counters, debug tables, debug-only globals), converted to `when (config::debug)` (design.md 12) |  |
| `kpp/when_object_lives_to_the_end_of_its_scope.kpp` | `when (condition) declaration;` (design.md 19) declares an object of the enclosing scope only when the condition holds: an RAII object's constructor runs at the declaration and its destructor at the end of the enclosing block, in reverse order with the block's other locals, as the C++ `#ifdef` declared it; when the condition is false there is no object, no constructor and no destructor | pass | the game's time measurers under #ifdef (`TimeMeasurer timeMeasurer(...)` for the rest of a function), converted from `#ifdef DEBUG` (design.md 12, "The game's DEBUG as config::debug") |  |
| `kpp/when_use_outside_its_condition_is_error.kpp` | a name `when (condition)` declares (design.md 19) is checked in the configuration being built: where the declaration does not exist (tracing is off here), a use of the name is a compile error in that build, never a silent fallback to another declaration of that spelling (here the library's count), while a use inside a discarded branch is not checked | pass | none: the converter writes each use of a debug-only local inside `if constexpr (config::debug)` or `when (config::debug)` |  |
| `kpp/when_use_outside_its_condition_where_it_exists.kpp` | a use of a `when` local is checked in the configuration being built (design.md 19): where the local exists (tracing is on here), a use outside any condition is an ordinary use of it (`4 3`) | pass | a debug-only local the converted code uses after its `#ifdef DEBUG`, in the debug configuration |  |

## C interop

| Snippet | Construct | Status | Factorio | Note |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `interop/c_anonymous_member_of_named_type.kpp` | MS C's anonymous member of a named struct type (`MONITORINFO;` inside MONITORINFOEXW, `SpecPoint2;`): as the first member, its members are the struct's and the layout is C's; anywhere else it is laid out, but its members are not reachable by their names | pass | GraphicsInterfaceDX11 and SDLWindow fill `MONITORINFOEX info; info.cbSize = sizeof(MONITORINFOEX);` for GetMonitorInfo: the import dropped the anonymous MONITORINFO (`MONITORINFO ;` read as a macro statement), so the struct was 64 bytes instead of 104 and cbSize no member (silent: GetMonitorInfo would have written past it) |  |
| `interop/c_callbacks.kpp` | kpp functions as C callbacks (function pointers with C signatures) and C function pointers stored in kpp | pass |  |  |
| `interop/c_class_through_varargs.kpp` | an object of a trivially copyable class passed through a C function's `...`, as MSVC passes one (C++ allows it): a class of 1, 2, 4 or 8 bytes by value, any other by the address of a copy; the C function's va_arg of the same struct reads it | pass | InputActionTestMaker's VariableToCode<QuickBarSetSlotParameters> and <QuickBarPickSlotParameters>, `ssprintf("...ItemStackIndex(%u))", param.targetQuickBarSlot)` with a 2-byte QuickBarSlotLocation, and PlatformBackdropCloud::parseCloudProjectionStyle's `ssprintf("\"%s\" is unknown ...", input)` with a std::string_view: wrong format arguments in the game (debug and error paths), which MSVC compiles |  |
| `interop/c_constant_through_function_macro.kpp` | an object-like macro whose body is a function-like macro's call on a constant is a constant too: SDL3's `#define SDL_WINDOW_FULLSCREEN SDL_UINT64_C(0x0000000000000001)` | pass | SDL's window flags at 6 uses (SDL_WINDOW_FULLSCREEN, SDL_WINDOW_MAXIMIZED, ImguiSDL's SDL_WINDOW_MOUSE_FOCUS) |  |
| `interop/c_declspec_align.kpp` | `__declspec(align(N))` on a C struct (winnt.h's DECLSPEC_ALIGN: CONTEXT, M128A) is its alignment: alignof is N, and a local of it lands on N bytes (RtlCaptureContext stores into a CONTEXT with aligned SSE moves) | pass | StackWalker::ShowCallstack's local `CONTEXT c; RtlCaptureContext(&c);` crashed with an access violation reading 0xffffffffffffffff when the game logged a stack: the import aligned CONTEXT to 8 |  |
| `interop/c_file_is_the_c_runtimes.kpp` | the standard library's FILE is the C runtime's own (stdlib/clib): on Windows the UCRT's `struct _iobuf`, one pointer, so sizeof(FILE) is C's 8, and a stream through FILE* writes and reads back | pass | the game's FILE* streams (fopen, fwrite, fprintf to stderr) and the converted C libraries' (zlib's gzip files, minizip's) |  |
| `interop/c_float_limits.kpp` | float.h's limits through the import: FLT_MAX, FLT_EPSILON, DBL_MAX, DBL_EPSILON | pass | 8 uses (ImguiSDL's `ImVec2(-FLT_MAX, -FLT_MAX)`, AsteroidCollectorNavmeshPathfinding's `float pathCost = FLT_MAX;`, RocketSilo's FLT_EPSILON) |  |
| `interop/c_header_inline_functions.kpp` | a function an imported C header defines inline (the UCRT's corecrt_math.h `__inline long double floorl(long double)`, `__inline float fabsf(float)`), which no library exports, is callable: the import compiles its body | pass | trio (converted as C): trio.c's trio_floor and trio_ceil are floorl and ceill (TRIO_FEATURE_FLOAT with long double), in TrioWriteDouble |  |
| `interop/c_import_anonymous_enum.kpp` | a C header's anonymous enum behind a typedef, `typedef enum { ... } CURLcode;`, as a type in kpp, also inside a struct's anonymous union (curl's CURLMsg.data.result) | pass |  |  |
| `interop/c_import_member_alignment_attribute.kpp` | `__attribute__((aligned(N)))` written after an imported C struct member's declarator aligns that member: the struct's size and alignment are C's, and so are its members' places (the C side reads what kpp wrote) (`96 16 96 16 16 80 4321`) | pass | none; kpp.net's FreeBSD 14.3 headers: machine/ucontext.h's `long mc_fpstate[64] __attribute__((__aligned__(16)))` and struct sigcontext's sc_fpstate were dropped, so ucontext_t was 360 bytes, not 880 (B's review of I's FreeBSD target) |  |
| `interop/c_import_nullability_restrict_noreturn.kpp` | clang's nullability qualifiers (`_Nonnull`, `_Nullable`, `_Null_unspecified`), C99's `restrict` before a parameter's name and C11's `_Noreturn` in an imported C header change nothing in a declaration: a struct keeps every member (its layout is C's) and the functions are declared (`32 10 12`) | pass | none; kpp.net's FreeBSD 14.3 headers have them: stdio.h's FILE (`int (* _Nullable _close)(void *)`), pthread.h's `pthread_mutex_t * restrict __mutex`, stdlib.h's `_Noreturn void exit(int)` (B's review of I's FreeBSD target) |  |
| `interop/c_import_stdlib.kpp` | importing C standard headers and using their functions, types and callbacks (qsort) | pass |  |  |
| `interop/c_import_unreadable_enumerator_is_not_declared.kpp` | an imported C enum with an enumerator whose value the import could not read is not declared, none of its enumerators (the parser drops the whole declaration): a use of one is an error, never a value other than C's (the next ones count on from the one it could not read) | pass | none; B's review of I's FreeBSD target (the import skipped what it could not read in struct bodies; an enum's error drops the enum) |  |
| `interop/c_import_unreadable_member_is_error.kpp` | an imported C struct whose definition the import could not read in full (a member in a construct the C parser does not read) is an error where a use needs its layout, and so is a struct that holds one; never a struct without that member, whose layout would silently be another than C's (a pointer to it needs no layout) | pass | none; kpp.net's FreeBSD 14.3 headers: mcontext_t's `long mc_fpstate[64] __attribute__((__aligned__(16)))` was skipped, so ucontext_t was 360 bytes, not 880 (B's review of I's FreeBSD target) |  |
| `interop/c_import_windows.kpp` | importing a C header (`extern "C" import "windows.h";`) and calling into it | pass |  |  |
| `interop/c_macro_casts.kpp` | an imported header's constant macro that casts through typedef names (windows.h's `INVALID_HANDLE_VALUE ((HANDLE)(LONG_PTR)-1)`) is the constant the casts make: all ones, as Win32 returns it | pass |  |  |
| `interop/c_math_classification_macros.kpp` | math.h's classification macros are usable through the C import as C uses them: fpclassify, signbit, isnan, isinf, and the FP_* constants the UCRT defines (FP_NORMAL is (-1), FP_SUBNORMAL (-2)) | pass | trio (converted as C): trionan.c's c99_fpclassify_and_signbit, which its `#if defined(PREDEF_STANDARD_C99) && defined(fpclassify)` selects in the C build, `switch (fpclassify(number)) { case FP_NAN: ... case FP_SUBNORMAL: ... }` |  |
| `interop/c_object_with_vtable.kpp` | a single-inheritance class's vtable is laid out as MSVC's and COM's: the object's first word points at the first virtual function's slot, the slots follow in declaration order, `this` is the first argument; so kpp calls a C object's table of functions through a class declaring the same virtual functions, and C calls a kpp object's through its table | pass |  |  |
| `interop/c_short_int_spellings.kpp` | a C header's `short int`, `unsigned short int` and `signed short int` are 16 bits, in a typedef, a struct's members and an inline function's parameter, result and cast (`long int` stays 4 bytes, as MSVC's long) | pass | none; glibc's `typedef unsigned short int uint16_t`, so ntohs and htons returned 32 bits on Linux (thread G, libraries/net) |  |
| `interop/c_static_inline_function_declared_extern_c.kpp` | a C function a header defines static inline and kpp declares extern "C" with its parameters is one function (C11 6.2.2p4: an extern declaration after a visible static one takes its linkage), not two overloads whose calls are ambiguous; the stdlib's CTime.kpp declares time, localtime, gmtime and mktime so, which MSVC 14.44's time.h defines static inline | pass | Lua's lstate.kpp `time(nullptr)`, PerformanceLogger's `::time(nullptr)` and localtime, minizip's mktime: "ambiguous between int64(int64*) (stdlib\clib\CTime.kpp:18) and int64(int64*) (<imported C headers>)" (headline 15) whenever the C imports read MSVC 14.44's headers (a VsDevCmd environment) |  |
| `interop/c_struct_member_attributes.kpp` | an imported struct with an attribute on a member (`long state[4] __attribute__((__aligned__(16)))`) or a nullability qualifier (`int (* _Nullable close)(void*)`) has C's layout, every member after it included | pass |  |  |
| `interop/c_structs_by_value.kpp` | a C function's struct by value, argument and result, passed as the target's C passes it (MS x64: a struct of 1, 2, 4 or 8 bytes in a register, others through memory; SysV x86-64: up to 16 bytes in INTEGER or SSE registers by its eightbytes, others on the stack, and on the stack whole when its registers ran out), against clang's C (c_structs_by_value.c); before 2026-11-01 kpp passed every struct as a pointer to a copy, MS x64's way only above 8 bytes | pass | the C libraries' small structs by value (SDL_Color, SDL_FPoint, lua_Debug fields are pointers) |  |
| `interop/c_structs_layout.kpp` | C-compatible layout: plain structs passed to and filled by C, sizeof agreement, arrays of structs | pass |  |  |
| `interop/c_typedef_names_of_anonymous_struct.kpp` | a C header's struct without a tag behind several typedef names, `typedef struct { ... } DNS_KEY_DATA, *PDNS_KEY_DATA, DNS_DNSKEY_DATA, *PDNS_DNSKEY_DATA;`: every name is the one type (the pointer names pointers to it), also as a member's type in another struct's union | pass | SocketAddress.cpp's DNS_RECORD (windns.h), whose union's `DNS_KEY_DATA KEY, Key;` made its layout fail: only the last name of such a typedef is declared |  |
| `interop/c_varargs_definition.kpp` | a C-variadic function defined in kpp reads its arguments as C does, with <stdarg.h> imported: va_list, va_start, va_arg (an int here), va_end, and a va_list handed on to vsnprintf | pass | Lua's luaO_pushvfstring (`va_arg(argp, char *)`, `va_arg(argp, int)`, `va_arg(argp, l_uacNumber)`, `va_arg(argp, void *)`), lua_pushfstring, luaL_error and luaG_runerror, all with `#include <stdarg.h>`; the game's own vssprintf passes a va_list on, with MSVC's from <stdio.h> |  |
| `interop/calling_convention_pointers.kpp` | function pointer types with a Windows calling convention: `BOOL(__stdcall*)(void)`, `VOID(NTAPI*)(DWORD)` (one convention on x64; the word is accepted and ignored) | pass |  |  |
| `interop/com_query_interface_template.kpp` | a COM interface class as generate_com.py writes them: IUnknown's virtual QueryInterface(const IID*, void**) next to unknwn.h's member template QueryInterface(Q** pp), which asks for Q's id (Q::iid(), each interface's static, a template so that only an interface asked for needs its id at link time) and is found through a derived interface's pointer | pass | GraphicsInterfaceDX11's `this->dxgiAdapter->QueryInterface(&adapter)` with a CComPtr<IDXGIAdapter3> (4c178a5b915) |  |
| `interop/extern_c_declaration_then_definition.kpp` | a function declared extern "C" and later defined without it has C linkage throughout (the first declaration's linkage, as in C++): the definition is the symbol the calls name | pass | Lua's lapi.c (converted): `extern "C" TValue *index2addr (lua_State *L, int idx);` then its definition; the Lua compatibility build failed with "use of undefined value @index2addr" |  |
| `interop/extern_c_declarations.kpp` | hand-written extern "C" declarations, passing Strings and buffers to C | pass |  |  |
| `interop/extern_c_variables.kpp` | a variable that an imported C header declares extern is the C library's variable: kpp reads the library's values (an array of structs, an int, a const int) and its writes are seen by the library's own code | pass |  |  |
| `interop/extern_variable_declaration.kpp` | an `extern "C"` variable declared in kpp source without an initializer refers to the C library's or the linker's (`extern "C" const IID IID_IDXGIDevice;`, here the linker's __ImageBase, the image's DOS header) | pass | the IIDs __uuidof names (IID_ID3D11Device, IID_IDXGIFactory1, ...), defined in dxguid.lib, declared by the kpp COM interfaces |  |
| `interop/kpp_declaration_of_an_imported_c_name.kpp` | a kpp declaration of a name an imported C header declares too (a type, a constant, an enumerator; not the header's own entity: a function it declares or an overload of it, a definition of its extern variable, an alias of its type) is a clash: the two are other entities (other layouts), and neither silently takes the other's place in any file | pass | fontstash (converted as C): stb_truetype.h's fallback rect packer (its own stbrp_context, stbrp_node, stbrp_rect, stbrp_init_target, stbrp_pack_rects, private to fontstash.cpp) took the place of stb_rect_pack.h's, which TextureProcessor imports: TextureProcessor's stbrp_context was stb_truetype's 20 bytes, not stb_rect_pack's |  |
| `interop/kpp_definition_of_a_struct_a_c_header_defines_and_names.kpp` | a kpp struct of the name of a struct an imported C header both defines and names with a typedef (`struct X {...}; typedef struct X X;`, `typedef struct X {...} X;`) is a clash: the header's typedef, which names the kpp struct as well, does not make the two one entity | pass | none; found beside interop/kpp_declaration_of_an_imported_c_name, where checking the header's typedef alone let the kpp struct take the header's place (sizeof 4, not 40) |  |
| `interop/library_win32_declarations_as_in_windows_h.kpp` | the library's Win32 functions (namespace win32) have windows.h's types: each is the same C | pass | the game imports windows.h beside the library (its threads, clocks and file system) |  |
| `interop/msvc_intrinsics.kpp` | MSVC's intrinsics that are instructions, as MSVC and clang-cl give them: _byteswap_ushort/ulong/uint64, the rotations (_rotl, _rotr, _lrotl, _lrotr, _rotl8, _rotr8, _rotl16, _rotr16, _rotl64, _rotr64; the count taken modulo the width, a negative one rotating the other way), __popcnt16/__popcnt/__popcnt64, _BitScanForward/Reverse(64) (with a zero mask: 0 and the index left as it was, which MSVC calls undefined) and __cpuid/__cpuidex | pass |  |  |
| `interop/sdl_header.kpp` | importing SDL3's header (the game's platform layer) and using its types and constants without linking it | pass |  |  |
| `interop/sse_auto_locals.kpp` | an `auto` local initialized from an SSE operation has the operation's vector type (__m128i of _mm_loadu_si128, __m128 of _mm_shuffle_ps with _MM_SHUFFLE), as when the type is written | pass | YCoCgDXTCompression's GetMinMaxYCoCg_SSE2, ScaleYCoCg_SSE2, EmitAlphaIndices_SSE2 and EmitColorIndices_SSE2 (`auto xmm0 = _mm_loadu_si128((const __m128i*)(colorBlock));`) and CpuIntrinsics::sum (`auto y = _mm_shuffle_ps(x, x, _MM_SHUFFLE(1, 0, 3, 2));`) |  |
| `interop/sse_lane_operations.kpp` | the SSE operations on 8-, 16- and 64-bit lanes and on the lowest lane, packs, saturating sums, sums of absolute differences, the set, load and store forms, conversions, the rounding mode, a prefetch, and MSVC's _BitScanReverse and __cpuid | pass |  |  |
| `interop/sse_scalar_conversions.kpp` | the SSE conversions of the lowest lane to an integer, as MSVC and clang-cl give them: _mm_cvtss_si32/_mm_cvtsd_si32 (and the 64-bit forms) round by the current rounding mode, to nearest even by default (2.5 to 2, 3.5 to 4); _mm_cvttss_si32/_mm_cvttsd_si32 truncate; a value out of range gives the integer indefinite (INT_MIN), never a wrong number | pass |  |  |
| `interop/sse_vector_values.kpp` | SSE vectors (__m128, __m128i) are values with intrin.h imported: locals, parameters, results and class members hold them, the _mm_ operations take and give them, and a wrapper class converts from and to them | pass |  |  |
| `interop/stdout_is_file_pointer.kpp` | stdin, stdout and stderr are FILE*, as in C: stdout passes to a FILE* parameter (a stream class built from it) and to the C functions that take FILE* | pass | StdoutWriteStream's `: FileWriteStream(stdout)` through the protected `FileWriteStream(FILE* fp)`: the library's stdout was a void*, no constructor took it, and the first run of the converted game stopped at the stub of StdoutWriteStream's constructor (built at startup for `StdoutWriteStream::instance`) |  |

## expressions

| Snippet | Construct | Status | Factorio | Note |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `expressions/noexcept_operator.kpp` | the noexcept operator is false when its operand can throw ([expr.unary.noexcept]): a call of a function not declared noexcept, a throw expression; true otherwise (a call of a noexcept function, arithmetic); a constant expression, so static_assert reads it | pending | none in the converted game or the stdlib (only noexcept specifications); found reviewing the parser's C-style casts: `noexcept(expr)` was parsed as `true` whatever the operand | the noexcept operator is a parse error ("not supported yet"); it was `true` for every operand; C:\kpp-sandbox\worktrees\codegen\compiler\tests\spec\expressions\noexcept_operator.kpp:9: error: the noexcept operator is not supported yet (at 'noexcept') |
| `expressions/address_of_reference_cast_to_second_base.kpp` | the address of `static_cast<B&>(d)` with B a base of D that is not at offset 0 (D : A, B) is the address of d's B part, as `B& b = static_cast<B&>(d)` binds it; casting back down with `static_cast<D&>(b)` gives d again | pass |  |  |
| `expressions/ambiguous_call_is_error.kpp` | a call that two overloads take equally well is an error, as in C++, never one of them taken silently: pick(int64, int32) and pick(int32, int64) given two int32s | pass |  |  |
| `expressions/assignment_evaluates_right_operand_first.kpp` | C++17's sequencing (design.md, "Evaluation order"): an assignment's right operand, = and every compound one, built-in or a class's operator, is evaluated before its left ([expr.ass] 1, [over.match.oper] 2); a subscript, a shift, .* and ->*, and a call (the callee, the object of a member call) evaluate their left operand first ([expr.sub], [expr.shift], [expr.mptr.oper], [expr.call] 8); the expected line is clang 19's | pass | CompiledMapGenSettings::createContext's `ctx.numericConstants[key] = *value;` with value a pointer into the same FlatMap: the left operand's insertion shifted the entries before *value was read, so "numeric-constant" got its neighbour's 1 instead of starting_area_radius's 150 (CompiledMapGenSettings/NoiseExpressionCompilationContext, the one failing unit test of 4c178a5b915) |  |
| `expressions/assignment_right_operand_first_closure.kpp` | an assignment whose right operand, evaluated first, is a lambda's closure holding what cannot be copied (`h->fn = [q = move(p)]() mutable {...}`, the left operand a call of SharedPtr's operator->) moves the closure into the Function, never copies it | pass | stdlib Future.kpp's async, `state->deferred = [raw, callable = move(callable)]() mutable {...}` (ForkTask, ThreadedChunkedZipPackageWriteStream): found making the right operand of an assignment evaluate first |  |
| `expressions/assignment_right_operand_first_keeps_value_category.kpp` | an assignment's right operand evaluated before its left keeps what it is: a temporary or an xvalue is moved from (operator=(T&&)), an lvalue, a call returning T& included, is copied; a free operator+= and an enum's operator|= take it as well; an assignment used as an lvalue (`int& r = (values[nextIndex()] = right());`) evaluates its left operand once; and the left operand's evaluation sees what the right one did (an insertion at the front that moves the element the right operand read); the expected line is clang 19's | pass | the pattern of CompiledMapGenSettings::createContext's `ctx.numericConstants[key] = *value;` (a FlatMap insertion moving the entry value points to) |  |
| `expressions/base_pointer_beats_void_pointer.kpp` | a pointer to a class converted to a pointer to its base is better than one converted to void* (C++ [over.ics.rank] 4.4.2): info(const Base*) over info(const void*) given a const Derived*, whichever is declared first, by value and by reference | pass | OpenableObjectWrapper's getPrototypeInfoStatic(const PrototypeBase*, ...) beside getPrototypeInfoStatic(const void*, ...) given each prototype type (29 calls), and makeExtra(const Sprite*) beside makeExtra(const void*) given a const LayeredSprite* |  |
| `expressions/c_cast_of_functional_cast.kpp` | a C-style cast to a type named by an alias or a class, `(S64)`, casts a functional cast or brace initialisation that follows it, whatever starts it: a builtin type `(S64)char(7)`, `(S64)unsigned(9)`, `decltype(x)(y)`, `typename T::value_type(v)`, a class `(S64)Meters{5}.value`; and an `alignof` expression; none of these can continue a parenthesised expression | pass | D's value corpus (generated expressions checked against clang): 42 cases of `(S64)char(7)` and its kin failed to parse ("expected ';' after declaration") |  |
| `expressions/c_ellipsis_is_the_worst_conversion.kpp` | matching a C ellipsis (`f(...)`, `f(int, ...)`) is worse than any standard or user-defined conversion ([over.ics.rank] 2): an int takes `f(FromInt)` (a converting constructor), `f(double)` and `f(const Base&)` for a Derived over `f(...)`; clang 19 prints `1 2 2 2` | pass | found by the templates thread comparing overload choices against clang 19 on generated calls: the ellipsis ranked as a standard conversion, so `f(...)` won against a user-defined one and tied with the others (silent and loud) |  |
| `expressions/conditional_converts_pointer_operand_to_class.kpp` | a conditional expression whose operands are a String and a `const char*` converts the pointer operand to String (the only direction an implicit conversion goes, C++ [expr.cond]): `none ? none->name : Mod::CORE_MOD` is the String "core" when none is null, passed to a `const String&` or used to initialize a String | pass | LuaGameScript::luaGetScriptInventories' `pushInventories(item.first ? item.first->id.name : Mod::CORE_MOD, &item.second)`: the core inventories went in under an empty name, so `game.get_script_inventories().core` was nil (LuaInventory LuaScriptInventoryOwnership) |  |
| `expressions/conditional_of_derived_and_base_is_the_base_part.kpp` | `c ? both : alone` with lvalues of a class Both and of its second base Second (Both : First, Second) is an lvalue of Second: in the first branch, both's Second part, read and assigned through a `Second&` bound to the conditional; and `c ? none : undo` with an Optional<Undo> and an Undo lvalue is a new Optional<Undo>, the Undo converted to it (C++ [expr.cond] 4); a base never converts to the derived class through the derived's converting constructor: `c ? negated : plain` with Negated deriving from Expression and `implicit Negated(const Expression&)` is plain itself when c is false (NoiseExpressions::Folding::subtract) | pass |  |  |
| `expressions/conditional_operands_take_usual_arithmetic_conversions.kpp` | a conditional whose operands are arithmetic or unscoped enumerations of two types has the type the usual arithmetic conversions give ([expr.cond]/7.2), not the first operand's: `c ? U200 : 0.1f` is a float (0.1), `c ? uint8 : char` an int, `c ? float : double` a double; one type cv-qualified differently (a `const uint16` lvalue and a `uint16` prvalue) stays that type; clang 19 prints `4 0.1 4 -7 8 0.5 2 9` | pass | found by the templates thread comparing values against clang 19 on generated expressions: the first operand's type took the other's value (`c ? U200 : 0.1f` gave 0), and a const operand beside a plain one of its type made an int (TagManager's `player ? player->index : PlayerIndex(-1)`) |  |
| `expressions/enum_promotes_to_its_fixed_underlying_type.kpp` | an unscoped enumeration with a fixed underlying type promotes to that type (C++ [conv.prom] 4), which beats a conversion to another integer type: push(const int32&) beside push(const uint32&) given an `enum LuaEventType : uint32_t` takes the uint32 one; one without a fixed type promotes to int32 | pass |  |  |
| `expressions/enum_with_fixed_underlying_type_promotes_as_it.kpp` | an unscoped enumeration with a fixed underlying type promotes as that type does ([conv.prom]/4): an `enum : long long` stays 64 bits in arithmetic (`SM + 1` is a long long, `SM ^ 100u` a long long -97, not an unsigned int), an `enum : unsigned char` becomes an int (`U200 << 1` is 400, not 144); one without a fixed type is an int (MSVC's underlying type); clang 19 prints `8 -97 400 4 8` | pass | found by the templates thread comparing values against clang 19 on generated expressions: the resolver promoted every enumeration to int, codegen an `enum : uint8_t` to its uint8_t unpromoted (silent: other values) |  |
| `expressions/free_operator_against_builtin.kpp` | a free operator competes with the built-in operator on a class operand's conversion as C++ ranks them, operand by operand: `n + 1.0` with `operator+(const Other&, const Other&)` reachable through converting constructors is the built-in addition (its 1.0 needs no conversion), `-n` with `operator-(const Num&)` the free one (it binds n as it is) | pass | none; B's review of the free unary operators (778f2ad0) |  |
| `expressions/free_operator_beside_conversion_is_ambiguous.kpp` | a free operator taking a class operand only by a user-defined conversion (another class's converting constructor) beside the built-in operator on the class's own conversion operator (an explicit operator bool for `!`) is ambiguous, as C++ ranks the two: an error, neither one called | pass | none; B's review of the free unary operators (778f2ad0), which called the free one |  |
| `expressions/free_unary_operator.kpp` | a unary operator declared as a free function (`Coin operator-(const Coin&)`, `bool operator!(const Flag&)`) is called for a class operand without one of its own, its operand evaluated once | pass | none (the game's unary operators are members); found with hidden friends, which are free functions |  |
| `expressions/function_declared_twice_is_one_candidate.kpp` | a function declared more than once is one candidate, so a call of it is no ambiguity: a declaration and then its definition, a namespace's function declared in one block and defined in another, a member declared in its class and defined outside it, and a C function that a program declares again (the library declares strlen too) | pass |  |  |
| `expressions/function_pointer_of_other_type_is_error.kpp` | a pointer to a function initialises or assigns only a function pointer of its own type (no implicit conversion between function pointer types), as in C++ | pass | found checking the library's C declarations against windows.h: `void (*)(void**)` took the address of a `void (int*)` function |  |
| `expressions/integer_to_pointer_is_error.kpp` | an integer converts to a pointer without a cast only as the literal 0, the null pointer ([conv.ptr]/1): `int* p = 5;`, `int* q = n;`, `p = n;`, an int argument and an int returned for a pointer are errors; `int* z = 0;`, `z = 0;` and `(int*)n` are not | pass | found by the templates thread with expressions/null_pointer_argument_for_two_pointer_overloads_is_ambiguous: an initialisation and an assignment took any integer as an address (silent); an argument was codegen's unsupported construct |  |
| `expressions/literal_zero_through_pointer_constructor.kpp` | the literal 0 is a null pointer constant, so it reaches a class through a converting constructor taking a pointer, as nullptr does (a user-defined conversion): `f(FromText)` given 0 calls `FromText(const char*)` with a null pointer, and wins over a candidate 0 does not convert to; clang 19 prints `2 1` | pass | found by the templates thread comparing overload choices against clang 19 on generated calls (loud: no call) |  |
| `expressions/null_pointer_argument_for_two_pointer_overloads_is_ambiguous.kpp` | a null pointer constant (nullptr, the literal 0) converts to every pointer type alike, so a call with one and two pointer overloads is ambiguous, whatever their pointees' qualifiers or classes ([over.ics.rank]: no tie-breaker compares two null pointer conversions); and nullptr makes no bool argument ([conv.bool]: only `bool(nullptr)`); each is an error | pass | found by the templates thread comparing overload choices against clang 19 on generated calls: `pick(nullptr)` took pick(Base*) over pick(const Base*), `take(0)` take(int*), and `flag(nullptr)` passed false (silent) |  |
| `expressions/pointer_conversions_without_cast.kpp` | the pointer conversions C++ makes without a cast ([conv.ptr], [conv.qual], [expr.type]/4): adding const, to `void*` and `const void*`, from a derived class to its base (moved to the base part), `T* const*` to `const T* const*`, an array and a function to a pointer, nullptr; a comparison of two pointers compares them as their composite pointer type (`this != &other`, a `const Derived*` against a `Base*`) | pass | the checks of expressions/pointer_of_other_type_is_error must keep every one of these (ChartRenderer's `const DisplayPanel* const* panels = displayPanels.data()`, LogisticNetwork's `&robot == avoid`); clang 19 prints `1 1 1 2 1 1 1 1` |  |
| `expressions/pointer_of_other_type_is_error.kpp` | a pointer converts without a cast only as C++ converts it ([conv.ptr], [conv.qual]): a `float*` is no `int*`, a `const int*` no `int*`, an `int**` no `const int**`, a `Base*` no `Derived*`, in an initialisation, an assignment, an argument and a return alike; each is an error | pass | found by the templates thread with expressions/function_pointer_of_other_type_is_error: every one compiled, and the pointer was used as the other type (silent) |  |
| `expressions/pointer_to_bool_is_the_worse_conversion.kpp` | of two conversions of one rank, the one that makes a pointer (an array's or a function's too) or a member pointer a bool is the worse ([over.ics.rank] 4.1): a Derived* takes `const void*` or `const Base*` over bool, an array `void*` over bool; a member pointer converts to bool; a `const char*` is no `void*` argument, so `const void*` is the one ([conv.ptr]); clang 19 prints `2 1 2 1 2` | pass | found by the templates thread comparing overload choices against clang 19 on generated calls: each was ambiguous or no call (loud) |  |
| `expressions/pointer_to_less_qualified_target_is_better.kpp` | given a T*, an overload taking T* is better than one taking const T* (the same exact match, one qualification less: C++ [over.ics.rank] 3.2.5); ItemRequestProxy::getProxyForEntity(Entity*) beside getProxyForEntity(const Entity*) given &entity, also for static members and free functions | pass |  |  |
| `expressions/reference_binding_beats_qualification_conversion.kpp` | a reference binding is an identity, which beats a qualification conversion of a pointer passed by value ([over.ics.rank] 3.2.5): `f(const T&)` with T = Derived* beats `f(const T*)` with T = Derived given a Derived*, `g(T&)` with T = Base* beats `g(const T*)`, and a non-template `h(Base* const&)` beats `h(const Base*)`; clang 19 prints `2 2 2` | pass | found by the templates thread comparing overload choices against clang 19 on generated calls: the identity and the qualification conversion tied, and partial ordering took `f(const T*)` (silent) |  |
| `expressions/reference_bound_to_operator_prvalue.kpp` | a reference bound to what an overloaded operator* or operator[] returns by value (a pointer, an int: a prvalue) binds a temporary holding that value, as a call's `const K&` argument (Map's operator[] given `*targeter`) and as a local `const T&`; a returned reference still names the object itself | pass | TrainManager::setup's `this->railPlaceMap[*connection.rail].add(railSignal)`: Targeter's operator* returns the rail by value and Map::operator[](const K&) was passed that pointer as the key's address, so every signal went to one bogus entry, no rail segment ended at a signal, and loading the train-junction menu map failed ("found rail (straight-rail on {-227, 305}) already connected to segment 3372") |  |
| `expressions/sizeof_consteval.kpp` | sizeof_consteval | pass |  |  |
| `expressions/static_cast_to_reference_through_conversion_function.kpp` | `static_cast<T&>(e)` with e of a class type that converts to T& calls the conversion function, as `T& t = e;` does (C++: the cast is valid when that declaration is): `&static_cast<TrainStop&>(builder.station())` is the address of the TrainStop the builder converts to | pass | TestTrainPriorities' `stop = &static_cast<TrainStop&>(leg.station("A"));` with RailBuilder's `operator TrainStop&()`: the cast took the RailBuilder itself as the TrainStop, so setPriority ran on the builder's stack memory and faulted in Rail::RailConnection::getRails (TrainPriorities stops; its 12 tests pass with `TrainStop& stop = leg.station("A");`) |  |
